OpenMetal (InMotion Hosting) — Sales Intelligence Dossier

Prepared for: Supermicro Sales Team 1 (USA) · Officer US8664 Tuo Cheng · Date: 2026-08-11 Method: Researched through corporate history, financial and registry records, U.S. political leanings, legislative and policy positions, and the company's relationships with its customers. Privately held — evidence comes from state business registries, ARIN/PeeringDB, the operator's own published GPU catalogue and pricing, UCC filings, job postings, community forums and FEC records. Every fact carries its source + date inline. GAP = not found in verified material. No fabrication. Territory: Los Angeles, California — West Coast South California = T1 | T2. Team 1 can register directly. CRM status: Verified clean 2026-08-11 in salesleads Search (Type = All) — no lead, no account, no do-not-call record. Registrable by Team 1.

1. Bottom line

OpenMetal, Inc. is a separately-incorporated OpenStack private-cloud, bare-metal and GPU operator sitting inside the InMotion Hosting group, headquartered at 2 Constitution Drive, Suite 101, Virginia Beach, VA 23462 (site footer, carrying "2022-2026 © OpenMetal, Inc."; the identical address is ARIN-registered to InMotion Hosting, Inc. under org INMOT-1, registered 2008-06-03). It self-declares $6M ARR, targeting $18M, on its own live careers page (OpenMetal careers) — the single most valuable and best-sourced commercial datapoint in this file, because it is a first-party disclosure rather than an aggregator estimate.

This is an existing Supermicro account that may already have been lost on the current generation, and the loss appears to have gone unnoticed. OpenMetal's own live website still states that its XL v2.1 and XL v3 bare metal servers "are provisioned on Supermicro's SYS-120C-TN10R" (XL v2.1, XL v3; page metadata dateModified 2026-01-23, so the claim is still being maintained), and Supermicro's own published case study (© 2021) names MBE-628E-822 MicroBlade enclosures with MBI-6219B-T41N blade nodes powering InMotion's Flex Metal Cloud, the direct predecessor of OpenMetal (Supermicro case study PDF). But a direct negative test against the raw HTML of the XL v5 page, the H200 GPU page, the GPU pricing page and the complete hardware index returns zero occurrences of "Supermicro" and zero "SYS-" model strings, against four occurrences plus the model number on each of v2.1 and v3. They named us for two generations and have named nobody since v4. That single asymmetry is simultaneously the opportunity, the risk, and the first question to ask.

Two things must be cleared before anyone dials. First, the channel. A customer with a published Supermicro case study is almost certainly served through a distributor or reseller, and the 2021 MicroBlade footprint implies an incumbent channel partner. The CRM was verified clean for "OpenMetal" — but the 2021 case study was published under InMotion Hosting, and the two entities share a street address, a NOC phone block and a BGP path. A clean search on one name proves nothing about the other. Second, the territory. The brief places this account in Los Angeles / T1; the evidence does not support that. Los Angeles is a leased Digital Realty LAX10 cage with no current-generation GPU in it; the corporate decision centre, the CTO, the CFO, the CLO, the President, the GPU fleet and the network all sit in Virginia. See the territory warning in §2 and §14 — escalate before registering.

Commercially, right-size it. This is a repeating 3-to-12-node buyer with long price-lock tails (bare metal pricing FAQ: "we typically can deploy up to 12 of a particular type in a particular data center at any given time"; initial deployments "can be of any size but 3 and 6 are common"), not a hundred-node tender, and nothing in the public record supports a $100M+ characterisation. Its value is annuity plus reference: one host platform spans Medium v5, Large v5, XL v5 and both GPU SKUs, so a single platform standardisation win captures the whole catalogue — and losing the platform loses all of it at once.

⚠️ Territory warning — read before registering

The Los Angeles / T1 premise is not supported by what was found, and a T1 registration on this account looks contestable.

  • Los Angeles is a leased colocation footprint, not an office and not the HQ. OpenMetal is a tenant at Digital Realty LAX10 (facility specs). The GPU servers this pursuit is about are currently deployed only in Ashburn, Virginia (GPU pricing page: "The RTX PRO 6000 and H200 are available now from OpenMetal US East in Ashburn, Virginia").
  • The corporate decision centre is Virginia Beach, VA. HQ address, every press-release dateline, and the President, CTO, CFO and CLO all sit East Coast. The network terminates in InMotion's Virginia NOC.
  • The genuine California nexus is narrow but real: (a) a second, older ARIN org "InMotion Hosting" (handle INMOT) registered 2004-06-14 at 2800 28th St., Suite 321, Santa Monica, CA 90405; (b) Sash Ghosh, Head of Growth and Partnerships, is Santa Monica, CA per her own FEC filings and carries a (323) LA-area phone on OpenMetal press releases; (c) InMotion Hosting's first data center was in Los Angeles in 2001 (Todd Robinson profile).
  • Action: re-run the territory test against the Virginia Beach HQ before registering. This reads as a T2/East-Coast-owned account with a California satellite, and a T1 registration may be challenged.

3. Corrections to the working list

Verdict scale: confirmed = first-hand named disclosure or multiple independent corroboration · partly confirmed = the core is true but the framing needs fixing · contradicted = evidence runs against the working list · unverified = cannot be established from public sources and must not be stated to the customer.

Rows 3 to 9 and row 14 correct this dossier's own earlier pass, not the original brief. They are recorded here rather than quietly overwritten, because four of them change how this account is actually worked — in particular the contact-route correction in row 3, which reverses the earlier conclusion that no individual at OpenMetal publishes a personal email.

4. Leadership & ownership

Evidence grades used in this section: primary-record = internet-number registry, corporate registry, campaign-finance filing, or the company's own published page · corroborated = two or more independent sources agree · single-source = one source only, no corroboration · GAP = nothing found after a named search.

Four framing facts before the table.

First — the corporate relationship is now settled, and it decides who signs. OpenMetal, Inc. is not the same legal entity as InMotion Hosting, Inc. and not merely a brand. It signs its own legal instruments in its own corporate name (trademark notice, legal@openmetal.io); it holds its own ARIN organisation OPENM-7 and its own ASN AS400956 (RDAP), separate from InMotion's INMOT-1 and AS22611; it is the named registrant of openmetal.io, registered 2021-09-13, registrant org "OpenMetal, Inc." (RDAP); and it maintains its own POC handles OOT1-ARIN and OAT5-ARIN, both captioned "OMI … (OpenMetal, Inc.)". The best characterisation is a separately incorporated operating affiliate under common founder ownership, with an interlocking board and a shared CFO/Board Chair — functionally a subsidiary or sister company, not a JV (no third-party partner appears in any record) and not a d/b/a. The contracting counterparty on any paper is OpenMetal, Inc.

Second — the control linkage runs through two men, and one of them is the whole decision. The two companies share a single HQ (2 Constitution Drive, Suite 101, Virginia Beach, VA 23462), share their founder-president (Todd Robinson is President of OpenMetal and President + Board Member of InMotion Hosting), and — the decisive finding of this pass — share their money officer: Raymond "Ray" Meyers is simultaneously OpenMetal's "Chief Financial Officer and Head of Mergers and Acquisitions" and InMotion Hosting's "Chief Administrative Officer & Board Chair." For a capital-equipment purchase that collapses two approval layers into one signature. Group scale per InMotion's own About page: founded 2001 by Sunil Saxena and Todd Robinson, "over 170,000 customers", "founder-led and independent, with no private equity"; a further group subsidiary, Web Ventures, is disclosed on the same page.

Third — this account publishes far more individual contact detail than the earlier pass recorded. Ten of the twelve OpenMetal team profile pages carry a personal email in a mailto: href. That correction is row 3 of §3 and it is load-bearing for how the account is worked. The local parts do not follow one guessable rule — Rafael Ramos is rafaelh@, Madeleine Ethridge is madeleinef@ — so published addresses are used verbatim and never extrapolated.

Fourth — registry officers and network contacts are listed as rows in their own right and labelled as such. An ARIN OrgAdmin, an IRR contact or a registered agent is a named human with a legitimately public route. On this account, however, every single ARIN and PeeringDB contact for both companies is a ROLE ACCOUNT — not one individual engineer is named in any network registry. The only genuine registry-officer names obtained anywhere are on the California record for INMOTION HOSTING, INC., and no registry record for OpenMetal, Inc. itself was obtained from any state (§4.2).

4.3 Buying committee

The shape of this committee changed materially in this pass. It is not the flat OpenMetal-only roster the earlier version described. It is two entities, one founder, and one man holding both the buying entity's finance chair and the parent's board chair — plus two InMotion-side operators who a purely OpenMetal-facing campaign would never reach.

Name Why they matter for a server purchase How to approach
Raymond "Ray" Meyers — CFO and Head of Mergers and Acquisitions (OpenMetal, Inc.) AND Chief Administrative Officer & Board Chair (InMotion Hosting, Inc.) THE SIGNATURE. This pass's central discovery is that one man holds the buying entity's finance chair and the parent's board chair at the same time. In a founder-led group with no private equity, a multi-node GPU or bare-metal fleet purchase needs exactly two things — a CFO who will fund it and a board that will bless it — and Meyers is both. He is also Head of M&A, so he is already the person who models multi-year asset economics rather than quarterly opex. Do not treat him as a back-office approver who ratifies an engineering choice; on this org chart he is the choice. Note too that InMotion has a separate CFO, Todd Bukovich, so a generic "reach the CFO" play lands on the wrong person and inside the wrong legal entity Go to him directly and early, at raym@openmetal.io — a first-party published address on his own profile page, not a guessed pattern. Lead with balance-sheet framing, not product framing: landed cost per GPU-node and per usable kW, depreciation schedule, residual value, and the gross-margin arithmetic behind OpenMetal's fixed-monthly-price thesis. Their whole commercial premise is that dedicated hardware at a fixed monthly rate beats metered hyperscaler pricing — which makes their margin literally the spread between hardware cost and rental price, so anything that credibly lowers acquisition cost expands the thesis they already sell. Because he wears the M&A hat, a financing, staged-delivery or trade-in structure will get a more serious hearing from him than from a pure operator
Todd Robinson — President, OpenMetal, Inc.; Co-Founder, President and Board Member, InMotion Hosting, Inc. Owner-operator and the founder whose personal thesis the company sells. At a self-declared $6M ARR scaling to $18M inside a founder-led, PE-free group, a fleet purchase is a founder-level decision, and he sits on both sides of the OpenMetal/InMotion boundary. He is a genuine open-source ambassador — an OpenInfra Days 2025 speaker on OpenStack for AI workloads — and will engage on engineering substance rather than positioning Founder-to-principal, economics-led, and routed through the OpenStack/OpenInfra ecosystem rather than cold. His LinkedIn is company-published on OpenMetal's own careers page, which makes it a legitimate, verifiable route. TITLE HAZARD: three primary pages call him President and the careers page calls him "our CEO" — use President, which has the stronger support. Pair any approach to him with Meyers rather than treating them as alternatives; they are the two halves of one decision
James "Jamie" Tischart — Chief Technology Officer, OpenMetal, Inc. (appointed 2026-02-13) THE TIMING PLAY. Roughly six months into the job as of this writing, hired externally from Twilio where he was VP of Software Engineering. His stated mandate in the appointment release is technology strategy, product, and "operational excellence, reliability at scale". A new CTO with an explicit operational-maturity brief is the single most likely person in the company to re-examine an inherited hardware standard — and the incumbent relationship predates him entirely. This window will not stay open Engineer-to-executive, on platform and operational maturity rather than on price. His Twilio background means he thinks in reliability at scale and platform standardisation, not per-unit BOM. His LinkedIn is company-published on the careers page. CONTACT CAUTION: he is one of only two OpenMetal executives whose profile page publishes NO personal email — do not invent one from the pattern, because the pattern demonstrably breaks at this company (Rafael Ramos is rafaelh@, Madeleine Ethridge is madeleinef@). Route via Meyers, via sales@openmetal.io, or via LinkedIn
Yuriy Shyyan — Director, Cloud Systems Architecture — with Jacob Hipps, Principal Engineer The technical qualification pair, and the gate a chassis must actually pass. Shyyan is described in OpenInfra materials as one of OpenMetal's original engineers and is an OpenStack Foundation member; Hipps is the Principal Engineer. Between them sits validation against OpenStack and Ceph, IPMI and BIOS access, PXE and immutable boot, Intel TDX/SGX enablement, and boot/data drive isolation — all requirements OpenMetal publishes openly. No commercial agreement survives their veto Engineer-to-engineer, leading with their own published non-negotiables rather than a datasheet: full IPMI and BIOS access; TDX enabled out of the box with no reconfiguration; boot/data drive isolation; and their unusual stated requirement of an attested GPU inside an Intel TDX trust domain — that last one is a genuinely differentiating platform question and the best single opener. Both publish personal addresses: yuriys@openmetal.io and jacobh@openmetal.io. Shyyan's LinkedIn is company-published on the careers page
Erik Soroka — Director of IT & Data Center Operations, InMotion Hosting, Inc. NEWLY IDENTIFIED IN THIS PASS AND PREVIOUSLY MISSING FROM THE BUYING PICTURE ENTIRELY. InMotion's own About page states the group designs, owns and operates its infrastructure "from the bare metal hardware to the private network it runs on" across "thousands of servers" in California, Virginia and the Netherlands. Soroka is the named director of that function. He owns the physical constraints that kill deals late — rack density, power and thermal envelope, remote-hands model, refresh timing — and he sits on the InMotion side, meaning a purely OpenMetal-facing campaign never reaches him Operations-to-operations, and deliberately early rather than late, so that power/thermal/rack fit is settled before a commercial proposal exists. GAP: no personal email is published for him; the only registry-published route into that function is the group NOC at noc@inmotionhosting.com / +1-757-693-5293. Treat reaching him as a discovery objective in its own right
Jason Loucks — GM, Cloud Division and Director of Development & Engineering, InMotion Hosting, Inc. NEWLY IDENTIFIED. A general manager carries a P&L, and his is the Cloud Division — the layer directly above cloud infrastructure spend — while he simultaneously runs development and engineering. He is the most likely person to hold a cloud-hardware budget on the parent's side. IMPORTANT AND HONEST CAVEAT: whether his "Cloud Division" scope formally includes OpenMetal, or only InMotion's separately-marketed InMotion Cloud / Managed Private Cloud product, is NOT stated in any source and must not be asserted. Both readings are plausible and the distinction changes who signs Treat him as a qualification question before treating him as a target: establish whether OpenMetal's infrastructure spend rolls into his division or sits entirely inside OpenMetal, Inc. Ray Meyers or Todd Robinson can answer that in one sentence, and the answer determines whether this is a one-entity or a two-entity sale. GAP: no published personal contact
Chris Gonzalez — Chief Legal Officer & General Counsel, InMotion Hosting, Inc.; registered agent of record for the California entity The counter-signature and paper gate. He is the group's only disclosed legal officer and, per the California registry, its agent for service of process — so terms, warranty, indemnity and delivery obligations route through him. He does not choose the hardware, but he can stall a signed decision for weeks Do not approach him first — engage him only once Meyers has said yes in principle, and then proactively, with clean paper. For the OpenMetal entity specifically the published legal route is legal@openmetal.io. NOTE FOR CONTRACTING: the counterparty on the paper should be OpenMetal, Inc. — the entity that executes its own legal instruments — not InMotion Hosting, Inc., and not "OpenMetal, a division of InMotion"
Dan Cunningham — Chief Revenue Officer & Board Member, InMotion Hosting — and the VACANT OpenMetal CRO seat A structural gap that is itself actionable. OpenMetal's CRO seat is empty and actively recruited on its own careers page, while InMotion has a sitting CRO who is also a board member. Capacity decisions — how fast new GPU capacity is filling, whether expansion is pipeline-backed — normally come from a CRO, and right now OpenMetal has nobody in that chair. That demand read is what converts a platform conversation into a dated order Two moves. First, get the demand signal from Mor Hanukaev (Director of Cloud Services, morh@openmetal.io) instead, since the CRO who would normally own it does not exist. Second, watch the CRO hire as a live trigger event — a new revenue leader arriving with a 3× ARR mandate is a buying-behaviour change worth timing an approach around. Do NOT assert that OpenMetal revenue currently escalates to Cunningham; that is a reasonable inference from the org chart but it is nowhere stated in the record

Productive. openmetal.io team profile pages — all twelve fetched individually and parsed for H-tags, meta descriptions and mailto: hrefs. Decisive for the "separate entity vs division" question. · openmetal.io privacy policy — corroborated "OPENMETAL, INC." as the executing entity with the Virginia Beach address. NOTE: /legal/terms-of-service/ returned HTTP 404 — the ToS is at a different path and no governing-law or state-of-incorporation clause was recovered. · ARIN Whois-RWS and RDAP — autnum 400956 established OpenMetal's own ASN (OPENMETAL-AS, 2024-04-04, org OPENM-7); autnum 22611 confirmed InMotion's (INMOTION, 2012-11-02, org INMOT-1); POC records OOT1-ARIN and OAT5-ARIN resolved to "OMI Operations Team" and "OMI Abuse Team (OpenMetal, Inc.)". · FEC OpenFEC API — highly productive after a workaround. The DEMO_KEY was already OVER_RATE_LIMIT (the same failure that truncated the earlier pass); resolved by using the public Schedule A key the FEC publishes in the source of its own fec.gov/data pages, with a fec.gov Referer. Yielded the full contributor_employer=OpenMetal index (7 records, 3 individuals) with the earmark memo_text fields that reveal the true recipients behind the ActBlue conduit, the contributor_employer=InMotion Hosting index (385 records), individual-name searches for 27 names, and committee/candidate profiles with treasurer names for all five recipient committees. · LDA API at lda.gov — the official Senate/House Lobbying Disclosure Act database; definitive negative (see §12). · Virginia Public Access Project — required a browser, since results are JS-rendered; yielded the vendor record and a clean OpenMetal negative. · PRNewswire — exact CTO appointment date, remit, direct quote, and a fourth title variant for Robinson ("Founder and President"). · RDAP for openmetal.io — registrant organisation and registrant state CALIFORNIA. · forebears.io — surname-origin entries for fifteen surnames, used strictly as name-etymology reference (see §12).

Best neutral next step to close the officer gap without touching the company: a paid registry pull or a human-operated browser session against the California SOS (bizfileOnline) and the Virginia SCC, searching both "OpenMetal, Inc." and "InMotion Hosting, Inc." The blocks are bot-detection and portal-availability problems, not paywalls of substance, and a single successful session would resolve state of incorporation, registered agent, officers and status — four of the largest gaps in this file — at essentially zero cost.

5. Footprint

All four sites are tenant-in-colocation. OpenMetal owns no data centre building. Every power and space figure below is a facility total published by OpenMetal about its landlord's building — OpenMetal's own cage size, rack count and contracted kW are not disclosed at any of the four sites and are a hard GAP.

OpenMetal's own page says services are "available within the LAX10 Digital Realty Data Center", and carries the explicit disclaimer "Listed certifications are held by facility operator and not necessarily OpenMetal*" — they are unambiguous that this is someone else's facility FACILITY TOTALS, NOT OPENMETAL'S FOOTPRINT: 7 levels; utility power capacity 27,000 kW; UPS power capacity 10,370 kW; UPS redundancy 2N; power density 250 W/sq.ft; generator capacity 18,000 kW; in-room cooling, heat rejection and cooling plant all N+1; DC power available; outside the 500-year flood plain; seismic Zone 4. Rationale: both verified data centres are tenant-in-colo at Digital Realty and NTT, so the assessed real property belongs to those landlords, not OpenMetal — an assessor search would return the operator, not the target. The one address where an assessor search would add value is the HQ at 2 Constitution Drive, Suite 101, Virginia Beach VA 23462, to establish whether the InMotion group owns or leases its head office. That remains a GAP. Court dockets were also not searched — GAP —

Geographic asymmetry — the commercially important reading. The 2025 GPU generation launched into US East AND US West simultaneously (2025-04-11 release). The 2026 generation launched into US East ONLY. Los Angeles LAX10, Amsterdam and Singapore are therefore three built-out sites with zero current-generation GPU, and the company has said in writing that more locations are planned. That is the near-term purchase — see §8.

6. Hardware fleet

Evidence grades used here: CONFIRMED = first-hand named disclosure or multiple independent corroboration · CIRCUMSTANTIAL = behaviour points but the vendor is never named · INFERRED = derived only from socket, form factor or catalogue structure · GAP = nothing found after a targeted, named search.

6.1 Vendor evidence

Vendor / category Evidence grade What the evidence actually says
Supermicro — for the v2.1 and v3 generations and the Flex Metal predecessor CONFIRMED — two fully independent primary sources (1) The CUSTOMER'S own live website names the exact SKU. Verbatim from openmetal.io page metadata (Yoast schema / meta description, dateModified 2026-01-23, so still being maintained): "OpenMetal's XL v2.1 bare metal dedicated servers are provisioned on Supermicro's SYS-120C-TN10R. with Intel Xeon Gold processors and 1TB RAM." and "OpenMetal's XL v3 bare metal dedicated servers are provisioned on Supermicro's SYS-120C-TN10R., dual Intel Xeon Gold 6430 processor, 1TB RAM." (XL v2.1, XL v3). (2) SUPERMICRO'S own published case study (© 2021) names MBE-628E-822 MicroBlade enclosures and MBI-6219B-T41N blade nodes powering InMotion's Flex Metal Cloud, the direct predecessor of OpenMetal: "InMotion Hosting is using Supermicro's MicroBlade to provide a high density, high performance, low cost, and power efficient platform for their IaaS architecture" (case study PDF). A joint PRNewswire release also exists: "Powered by Supermicro MicroBlade Servers, InMotion Hosting Delivers an On-Demand OpenStack Private Cloud that Deploys in Less Than an Hour". This is an established, mutually-publicised Supermicro account
Supermicro — for the CURRENT v4, v5 and GPU generations GAP — AND THIS IS THE SINGLE MOST IMPORTANT FINDING IN THE DOSSIER. Never state Supermicro is the current incumbent. This was explicitly tested against fetched raw HTML, not assumed. The raw HTML of the XL v5 page, the H200 GPU page, the GPU pricing page and the complete hardware index contains ZERO occurrences of "Supermicro" and zero "SYS-" model strings — while v2.1 and v3 each contain four occurrences plus the model number. Measured result: hw_v21 4 hits + SYS-120C-TN10R; hw_v3 4 hits + SYS-120C-TN10R; hw_v5 0 hits, no SYS- model; h200 0 hits, no SYS- model; hardware index 0 hits, no SYS- model. OpenMetal named its chassis vendor for v2.1 and v3 and stopped naming anyone from v4 onward. Three readings are possible and public sources cannot distinguish between them: (a) they switched vendors for v4/v5/GPU; (b) they still buy Supermicro but removed vendor branding from marketing copy as they matured the brand; (c) they now use a mix or an ODM. Resolve in the first conversation — see §14
NVIDIA CONFIRMED Current: H200 NVL (141 GB HBM3e) and RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell Server Edition (96 GB GDDR7), both named on the GPU pricing page and in the 2026-06-17 release, which also states a five-year NVIDIA AI Enterprise subscription is included with the H200. Prior generation: A100 and H100, named in the 2025-04-11 GA release. They also publish vGPU engineering notes referencing GRID driver installation and OpenStack Nova vGPU work presented at the OpenInfra Summit 2023
Intel CONFIRMED — and this is a strategic platform commitment, not a commodity choice Named CPU SKUs across four generations: Xeon 6338 (v2.1); Xeon Gold 6430 (v3); Xeon Gold 6530 / 6544Y / 6526Y, Silver 4510 (v4); Xeon 6530P / 6517P / 6505P — Granite Rapids on Intel 3 (v5, including the GPU hosts). They lean heavily on Intel TDX and SGX confidential computing as a differentiator — TDX "enabled out of the box" on XL v5 — and on Intel AMX for CPU inference. InMotion also announced an Intel Xeon Scalable dedicated-server upgrade in 2020 (hardware index, XL v5)
Micron CONFIRMED — a deliberate, specified component, not whatever the integrator shipped Micron 7500 MAX NVMe named as the standard data drive across the entire v4 and v5 catalogue and both GPU SKUs ("1x 6.4TB included, up to 8 NVMe bays (Micron 7500 MAX)"). Micron 7450 named on the earlier v2.1 and v3 generations. They publish drive-level performance claims ("1.1M random read IOPS per drive at sub-1 ms QoS") (hardware index)
Digital Realty (LAX10) and NTT (Ashburn VA1) CONFIRMED — as colocation landlords, not hardware vendors Included because they define the physical envelope any server sale must fit. NTT VA1 supports "up to 22 kW per rack" — the binding constraint on GPU density in their only current GPU site (LAX10, VA1)
Dell, Cisco, Samsung CIRCUMSTANTIAL — DO NOT REPEAT AS FACT A search-result summary attributed to inmotionhosting.com content stated that InMotion's partners "include Intel, Cisco, Samsung, Dell, SuperMicro, and OpenStack Foundation". That primary page was NOT reached or verified, and it refers to InMotion Hosting broadly, not to OpenMetal's server fleet. If Dell is genuinely in the group's supply chain it materially changes the competitive picture, so it is worth verifying — but it is currently one unverified secondary citation (apparent origin, NOT FETCHED)

SECOND GPU SKU — "RP6000" / NVIDIA RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell Server Edition (current, GA 2026-06-17) 1 or 2× NVIDIA RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell SE, 96 GB GDDR7 per GPU, 24,064 CUDA cores per GPU. Same host platform as the H200 SKU: 2× Xeon 6530P (64C/128T), 1 TB DDR5-6400 to 2 TB, 1× 6.4 TB to 8 NVMe bays (Micron 7500 MAX), 20 Gbps private to 40 Gbps, 10 Gbps public GPU pricing page, verbatim; corroborated by the June 2026 release and HPCwire pickup The two GPU SKUs share one chassis design — a single platform win covers both product lines. They standardise the host and vary only the accelerator
PRIOR GPU GENERATION — NVIDIA A100 and H100 (GA 2025-04-11) "fully dedicated NVIDIA GPUs, including the A100 and H100 models" — "A100, H100 and more models for various performance and budget needs". Deployable as standalone nodes or clusters, integrated with OpenStack. Available at GA in BOTH U.S. East and U.S. West 2025-04-11 release, verbatim Legacy installed base and a refresh conversation. An A100 fleet bought pre-April-2025 is now several generations behind on memory bandwidth and is the natural displacement target inside their own estate. Note this generation reached the West Coast; the current one has not
BARE METAL — XL v5 (flagship CPU node, current generation) 2× Intel Xeon 6530P, 64C/128T; 1 TB DDR5-6400 with "16 of its 32 DIMM slots populated"; 4× 6.4 TB Micron 7500 MAX (25.6 TB); 2× 960 GB boot drives in RAID 1; 20 Gbps private LACP upgradeable to 40 Gbps. Intel TDX enabled out of the box; SGX also supported. Intel AMX for inference XL v5 hardware details and hardware index, verbatim Dual-socket Xeon 6 with 32 DIMM slots and 8+ NVMe bays. The "16 of 32 DIMM slots populated" detail matters — they buy half-populated memory with deliberate upgrade headroom, so DIMM-slot count is a real selection criterion and DDR5-6400 at 1DPC is required
BARE METAL — Large v5 and Medium v5 (current) Large v5: 2× Intel Xeon 6517P, 32C/64T, 512 GB DDR5-6400 (to 1 TB), 2× 6.4 TB Micron 7500 MAX (12.8 TB), 20 Gbps private to 40 Gbps, 190 W TDP per socket. Medium v5: 2× Intel Xeon 6505P, 24C/48T, 256 GB DDR5-6400 (to 1 TB), 1× 6.4 TB Micron 7500 MAX, 20 Gbps private to 40 Gbps hardware index and the 2026-05-15 supply-crisis post, verbatim ★ STRATEGIC READ — THE HIGHEST-LEVERAGE FACT IN THIS DOSSIER: OpenMetal runs ONE host platform across Medium / Large / XL / GPU. A single platform standardisation win therefore captures the entire catalogue, not one SKU — and losing the platform loses all of it at once
BARE METAL — v4 generation (Emerald Rapids, prior generation, still in catalogue) XXL v4: 2× Xeon Gold 6530, 64C/128T, 2 TB DDR5, 115.2 TB NVMe in a 3-node cluster. XL v4: 64C/128T Xeon Gold 6530, 1 TB DDR5-4800, 4× 6.4 TB Micron 7500 MAX (25.6 TB), 2× 960 GB boot RAID 1, 20 Gbps private LACP, 6 Gbps public uplink. XL v4 High-Frequency: 2× Xeon Gold 6544Y, 32C/64T, 1 TB DDR5-5200, 25.6 TB NVMe. Large v4: 2× Xeon Gold 6526Y, 32C/64T, 512 GB DDR5-5200. Medium v4: Xeon Silver 4510, 24C/48T, 256 GB DDR5-4400 hardware index plus the XL v4 pricing answer page (authored by Sash Ghosh, posted 2026-04-22), verbatim The v4 fleet is the refresh pipeline. It is still actively sold, so it is installed base being added to as well as aged — and per their own admission, TDX on v4 required upgrades that v5 gets for free
STORAGE NODES — Storage Large v4 / Storage Medium v4 Storage Large v4: Xeon Silver 4510, 24C/48T, 256 GB DDR4, 240 TB HDD + 25.6 TB NVMe, 20 Gbps private. Storage Medium v4: Xeon Silver 4510, 24C/48T, 256 GB DDR5, 120 TB HDD + 12.8 TB NVMe, 20 Gbps private hardware index, verbatim. Note their own index lists DDR4 for Storage Large and DDR5 for Storage Medium — possibly a site error, worth confirming High-density top-loading JBOD-style storage chassis (240 TB HDD implies roughly 12–16 large-capacity spindles plus a separate NVMe tier). A distinct chassis family from the compute line and a separate attach opportunity for a Ceph capacity tier
HISTORIC PLATFORM — Supermicro SuperServer SYS-120C-TN10R (v2.1 and v3) XL v2.1: dual Intel Xeon 6338, 1 TB RAM, 6.4 TB Micron 7450, on SYS-120C-TN10R. The SYS-120C-TN10R is a 1U dual Socket P+/LGA-4189 barebone, 860 W PSU, Intel C621A Verbatim from OpenMetal's own live page metadata, dateModified 2026-01-23. SYS-120C-TN10R is the 1U 10-bay NVMe Ultra platform. Their v5-generation successor in the same product position would be the Xeon 6 equivalent of this exact 1U dual-socket all-NVMe design
HISTORIC PLATFORM — Supermicro MicroBlade MBE-628E-822 + MBI-6219B-T41N (Flex Metal Cloud, the OpenMetal predecessor) MBE-628E-822 MicroBlade enclosure and MBI-6219B-T41N blade server nodes. Used to fit "an entire fully-featured OpenStack deployment on three Supermicro MicroBlade sled server nodes", scaling to "hundreds of servers, thousands of VMs, and petabytes of storage", with cloud core setup in under 45 minutes Supermicro's OWN published customer case study, © 2021, model numbers extracted directly from the PDF (case study) DIRECT — Supermicro's own reference account. There is an existing, publicly-celebrated Supermicro relationship with this customer group dating to at least 2021. Check for an existing account team, existing registrations and prior deal history BEFORE touching this
FLEET SIZE NOT DISCLOSED. No server count, GPU count, rack count or contracted kW is published anywhere Bounding evidence only: AS400956 announces 15,616 IPv4 addresses across 28 prefixes plus four IPv6 blocks (RIPEstat, measured 2026-08); the bare metal FAQ says "we typically can deploy up to 12 of a particular type in a particular data center at any given time" and that initial customer deployments "can be of any size but 3 and 6 are common"; NTT VA1 caps them at 22 kW per rack Right-size the pursuit accordingly. These figures describe a buyer transacting in units of roughly 3–12 nodes, not 100-node clusters. Expect small, repeated orders with a long price-lock tail, not a single large tender

Five surfaces were checked: the GPU pricing page, the bare metal pricing catalogue, the hardware index, the XL v4 pricing answer page, and the deployment-calculator page. OpenMetal has removed ALL hardware dollar pricing from its public site. Their own April 2026 pricing page says "Monthly Price: Contact for pricing." Any rent-derived cost ceiling therefore CANNOT be built on a published price — see §10 bare metal pricing · XL v4 pricing answer page

AI position, read plainly. They sell dedicated, non-metered, single-tenant GPU into "healthcare, finance, research, and SaaS that need data locality and compliance control", and they publish a dedicated guide for "Cloud Resellers Using OpenMetal's Private GPU Servers". A meaningful share of their GPU demand is therefore resell demand — other cloud and hosting providers building on top of them — which is lumpier and more price-elastic than end-user demand and should temper any forecast built off their ARR trajectory alone.

8. Purchase clock

How often OpenMetal actually buys new silicon, reconstructed from dated press releases, WordPress page metadata embedded in each hardware page's raw HTML, and Wayback CDX index queries. Read §8.4 before relying on any of it — the archive content diff the brief asked for could not be completed.

3 2022-03 "OpenMetal Cloud Expands Hardware Catalog and Cloud Features" (PRNewswire 301506970) and "OpenMetal Creates a New Cloud Segment with the First On-Demand, OpenStack Private Cloud" (PRNewswire 301502116) PRNewswire release IDs as cited; no direct URL captured — cited by ID only

8.2 Cadence — two separate clocks, and they are not synchronised

CPU / platform clock: v2.1 (2021–22) → v3 (2024-03) → v4 (2024–25) → v5 (2026 H1). That is roughly a 12–18 month generational cadence, tracking Intel's Xeon releases almost immediately — they moved to Granite Rapids within about 15 months of its datacenter launch. They are a FAST FOLLOWER on Intel silicon, not a laggard.

GPU clock: A100/H100 GA 2025-04-11 → H200 NVL + RTX PRO 6000 GA 2026-06-17. That is a single measured interval of 14 months. One interval is not a cadence — treat 14 months as indicative only, n = 1.

GEOGRAPHIC clock — the one that matters most here: the 2025 GPU generation launched into US East AND US West simultaneously. The 2026 generation launched into US East ONLY. The West Coast, EU and APAC are therefore all UNBUILT on the current GPU generation, and the company has said in writing that more locations are planned.

Last event: 2026-06-17 — GA of the H200 NVL and RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell GPU servers on the v5 platform, Ashburn VA. Announced from Virginia Beach, quoted by CTO Jamie Tischart, press contact Sash Ghosh (323) 301-7563. Approximately 8 weeks before this research date (2026-08-10 / 11).

8.3 Next window

PRIMARY WINDOW — Q4 2026 to Q2 2027, for GPU capacity in the regions that do not yet have it. This is the actionable one and it is nearer than a generational refresh. Reasoning: they explicitly published "additional locations planned" and "Contact us about availability in other regions"; the previous GPU generation shipped to US West on day one, so West Coast demand is proven; and LAX10 plus Amsterdam plus Singapore are three built-out sites with zero current-generation GPU.

SECONDARY WINDOW — H1 2027 for the next CPU platform step on the 12–18 month cadence from v5.

TERTIARY WINDOW — around Q3 2027 for the next GPU generation, if the single 14-month interval repeats.

Trigger events to watch, in priority order: 1. The vacant CRO being filled — the requisition targets $6M → $18M ARR, and a 3× revenue plan forces a capacity plan. 2. Any announcement of GPU availability outside Ashburn. 3. The next v-generation pre-order page going live — they pre-sell before shipping, and pre-order is when they commit to purchase volume.

8.4 Method — stated plainly, including its limits

Four techniques were used. (1) Dated primary press releases with explicit datelines — this carried most of the weight and is the most reliable. (2) WordPress structured metadata embedded in each hardware page's raw HTML — the datePublished, dateModified and _wp_old_date fields, which reveal when a SKU page was created and last touched independently of the visible copy. (3) Wayback CDX index queries against openmetal.io — 21 distinct content-digest snapshots of the GPU pricing URL between 2025-04-14 and 2026-07-05, plus a domain-wide URL inventory. (4) Cross-checking snapshot first-appearance against press-release dates.

★ IMPORTANT LIMITATION — THE WAYBACK DIFF THE BRIEF ASKED FOR COULD NOT BE COMPLETED. The CDX index API was reachable, but every attempt to retrieve archived page CONTENT failed: direct fetches of web.archive.org/web/<timestamp>id_/ returned HTTP 000 (blocked at the network layer) and WebFetch returned "Claude Code is unable to fetch from web.archive.org". I therefore have snapshot timestamps and content digests but not the archived HTML, so I could not diff SKU-by-SKU across 2023–2026 to date each GPU's first appearance from the archive itself. The generation dates above come from press releases and page metadata instead — a sound substitute, but a different method than requested.

Anyone re-running this from an unrestricted network should diff these captures directly: 20250414135244, 20250710103438, 20250903131124, 20251111134645, 20260115182859, 20260217050722, 20260423154428, 20260609113223, 20260705080531. The digest change between the 20260609 and 20260705 captures should bracket the 17 June H200/RP6000 addition.

Confidence: High on the 2025-04-11 and 2026-06-17 GPU events (dated primary press releases, one independently corroborated by a Wayback first-capture three days later). Medium-high on the CPU generational cadence (page metadata plus catalogue structure). Medium on the forward window, which rests on a stated intention ("additional locations planned") rather than an announced date. Low on the GPU cadence as a rhythm — n = 1.

9. UCC financing record

Scope of this track: OpenMetal, Inc. (openmetal.io, AS400956) — Virginia Beach, VA; OpenStack private-cloud, bare-metal and GPU operator. Parent entity that must be searched in parallel: InMotion Hosting, Inc. — same street address, ARIN org INMOT-1, and the more probable debtor of record (see §9.4).

9.1 Verdict

UNVERIFIED — portal blocked

Read that exactly as written. Zero UCC filings were retrieved, in any jurisdiction, for either debtor. Compounding the problem, the state of organization is itself unverified (§2), so even the correct jurisdiction to search is unknown — the candidates are Virginia (HQ/operating state) and Delaware (typical incorporation state). The empty filing set in §9.2 means nothing was seen. It does not mean OpenMetal has no secured debt. It must never be reported to a customer, quoted to credit, or entered into CRM as "no liens found." The gating here is procedural, not evidence of a clean balance sheet.

9.4 What the record means

Inference, not sighted evidence. Every row states its own confidence.

Observation What it implies Confidence Sales consequence
A blocked portal is silence, not a clean record. No filing number, date, lapse, secured party, debtor address, collateral description or amendment was obtained in any state, and the correct jurisdiction is itself unknown No conclusion about OpenMetal's secured debt can be drawn. Any representation that "OpenMetal has no liens" would be unfounded high — directly observed: HTTP 403 / 404 / SPA-shell / 401 across four surfaces, logged verbatim above Do NOT let anyone build a credit or channel-approval argument on "no UCC filings found." Clear it with a human browser session at the Virginia SCC before any Rule 8 conversation or direct-terms decision
★ SCALE AND PARENTAGE — the most important inference in this section. OpenMetal self-declares $6M ARR on its own careers page while sitting inside a privately-held group its own President describes as "125,000+ customers and 350+ team members" across InMotion Hosting, BoldGrid, Web Hosting Hub and RamNode A $6M-ARR unit inside a group that size does not typically raise its own asset-backed facility. The overwhelmingly likely mechanism is funding off the parent's balance sheet — in which case any UCC-1 would name InMotion Hosting, Inc. or a group holding entity as debtor, not OpenMetal, Inc. medium — the ARR and group-size figures are first-party disclosed; the funding-structure inference is commercial reasoning ★ A searcher who queries only "OpenMetal" will find nothing even on an unblocked portal. Any re-run must search both names. This is the single most likely way a future search produces a false negative
No external equity evidence. No funding announcement, no investor, no venture round was located. The About page frames the company as founder-built out of InMotion's operating experience Absent outside equity, hardware is funded from group cash flow, vendor terms, or a lessor medium — negative-search result; absence of a funding announcement is suggestive, not conclusive The funding question is genuinely open. Ask Raymond Meyers directly how the last GPU tranche was funded rather than assuming
A direct hint, carefully qualified. Their Los Angeles service page advertises a "Hardware monthly leasing model (1, 2, 3, 4, and 5 year price lock options)" That describes what they SELL to customers, not how they BUY. But a company whose entire revenue model is monthly hardware leasing with multi-year price locks has an obvious structural incentive to match-fund on the buy side — a five-year fixed-price commitment to a customer against unhedged hardware cost is balance-sheet risk. Their own 2026-05-15 post shows they think in exactly these terms medium — the price-lock language is directly sourced; the match-funding inference is commercial reasoning, not a sighted filing Expect financing structure to be a live topic, not an afterthought. If they match-fund, a vendor or lessor may already hold a perfected interest in the fleet, and a new purchase-money security interest would need PMSI mechanics planned before hardware ships, not after
Cost pressure is acute and self-admitted. They published that server DRAM rose ~95% in early 2026 and that "OpenMetal's new v5 hardware reflects current market pricing" A margin-squeezed operator on a fixed-price rental model is more likely, not less, to be receptive to financing structures medium-high — the quote is first-party and dated The financing conversation is an opening, not an obstacle. Bring terms as well as price
If a secured party is ever found, its identity is the tell A bank or independent lessor implies they buy direct with working capital → pitch direct terms. A distributor or vendor-finance arm implies they already transact through a channel → Rule 8 is largely pre-solved but the channel owns the relationship medium — industry inference Read the secured-party NAME first. It answers the channel question in §14 faster than any conversation will

9.5 GAP — UCC track, stated plainly

  • UCC IS UNVERIFIED, NOT CLEAN. Zero filings retrieved. No debtor-name query was ever submitted to any register. §9.2 is empty because nothing was seen — this must never be reported as "no financing."
  • The state of organization is unknown, so the correct jurisdiction to search is also unknown. Virginia (operating/HQ) and Delaware (typical incorporation) are both live candidates. Resolving the state is a prerequisite to a meaningful UCC search, not a separate task.
  • RE-RUN INSTRUCTION, EXPLICIT: from an unrestricted network, search both OPENMETAL, INC. and INMOTION HOSTING, INC., in both Virginia and Delaware. The parent is the more probable debtor name. Budget for name variants — exact/standard search logic means OPENMETAL INC and OPENMETAL, INC. can return different sets.
  • No registered agent, officer list, formation date or entity status was obtained either — the same portals gate both. See §4.2.
  • Court dockets were not searched. No PACER, no state court, no litigation history for OpenMetal, Inc. or InMotion Hosting, Inc. — GAP.

10. Cost ceiling

10.1 The method the brief specifies cannot be executed as written — and that is stated, not faked

★ OpenMetal publishes no hardware prices. None.

The rent-derived method requires a published monthly price. Five separate surfaces were checked and every one is priceless: the GPU pricing page (every SKU button reads "Contact Sales"), the bare metal pricing catalogue (no dollar figures in the rendered page or the raw HTML), the hardware index ("Contact OpenMetal for GPU pricing"), their own XL v4 pricing answer page dated 2026-04-22 ("Monthly Price: Contact for pricing"), and the cloud deployment calculator. The only dollar figure published anywhere on openmetal.io is $375/month per additional 1 Gbps of public egress. Historical prices could not be recovered either, because Wayback content replay was blocked (§8.4).

Everything in §10.2 is therefore built on an EXPLICIT ASSUMPTION, not a researched price, and must be labelled as such in any customer-facing document.

10.2 Rent-derived ceiling — assumptions first, arithmetic shown so it can be re-run

ASSUMED INPUT — rental band. A dedicated dual-H200-NVL bare metal node at fixed monthly rental in the US market plausibly rents in the band $4,500–$9,000/month. This was NOT sourced from OpenMetal. It should be replaced the moment a real quote is obtained — and obtaining one is cheap, because OpenMetal will issue a written quote and honours it for 30 days.

ASSUMED OPERATING-COST SHARE — 30–45% of gross rent, covering colocation power and space, bandwidth, support labour, platform overhead and margin. Set toward the higher end deliberately because OpenMetal is a colocation TENANT at Digital Realty and NTT with no owned-facility advantage, and because they absorb 20 Gbps of included private networking and bundled egress rather than metering it. That leaves 55–70% of rent available to service the hardware.

Scenario Monthly rent (ASSUMED) Opex share (ASSUMED) Recoverable / month Ceiling @ 12 mo Ceiling @ 18 mo Ceiling @ 24 mo
Low end $4,500 45% $2,475 $29,700 $44,550 $59,400
High end $9,000 30% $6,300 $75,600 $113,400 $151,200
Band $4,500–$9,000 30–45% $2,475–$6,300 ≈ $30k–$76k ≈ $45k–$113k ≈ $59k–$151k

INTERPRETATION — the one genuinely useful output. Only the 18- and 24-month columns comfortably clear the BOM figure in §10.3, which says their payback discipline on GPU nodes is almost certainly 18 months or longer. That is consistent with their own commercial design: they sell 1-to-5-year price locks, and their bare metal FAQ offers locks up to five years. A vendor conversation anchored on 12-month payback will not fit their model.

Assumptions and their confidence, stated so they can be challenged:

  • (a) The $4,500–$9,000/month rental band is ASSUMED, not sourced — the highest-uncertainty input in the whole model, and everything downstream scales linearly with it.
  • (b) The 30–45% operating-cost share is ASSUMED, set toward the high end for the colo-tenant reason above.
  • (c) Payback of 12/18/24 months is the brief's framing, not their stated policy. Their actual capital-recovery horizon is unknown and is a question to ask.
  • (d) The H200 SKU is ASSUMED to ship in its 2-GPU configuration. The catalogue offers 1 or 2 GPUs, and a 1-GPU node would roughly halve the accelerator cost while leaving host cost unchanged.
  • (e) The five-year NVIDIA AI Enterprise subscription is EXCLUDED from the hardware BOM even though the June 2026 release says it is included with the H200 — it is a software subscription, its OEM-bundled cost is not public, and including it would inflate the hardware ceiling misleadingly.
  • (f) The model ignores their financing structure, which is unknown because the UCC search was blocked (§9).
  • (g) 2026 pricing is unusually unstable by their own account, so any band computed today has a short shelf life — they themselves note quotes hold only 30 days and that new quotes reflect then-current market pricing.

10.3 BOM rebuild — one flagship node (2× H200 NVL configuration)

Only one line here is genuinely researched. The separation is explicit.

Component Detail Cost Sourcing status
2× NVIDIA H200 NVL 141 GB PCIe $28,000–$35,000 each street as of 2026, with multiple resellers clustering at $31,000–$32,000 $56,000–$70,000 (most likely ≈ $62,000–$64,000) ★ SOURCED. The only component price actually researched. This single line is 70–80% of the machine
2× Intel Xeon 6530P (Granite Rapids, 32C each) Host CPUs per OpenMetal's published spec ≈ $5,000–$8,000 the pair ASSUMED — DO NOT QUOTE AS FACT
1 TB DDR5-6400 RDIMM, 16 of 32 slots populated Per OpenMetal's own published population pattern ≈ $7,000–$12,000 ASSUMED — DO NOT QUOTE AS FACT. Deliberately skewed high because OpenMetal themselves published that server DRAM rose nearly 95% in early 2026
1× 6.4 TB Micron 7500 MAX NVMe Included drive per the published spec ≈ $1,000–$1,500 ASSUMED — DO NOT QUOTE AS FACT
Dual-socket Xeon 6 PCIe GPU chassis — motherboard, redundant PSUs sized for 2× 600 W cards, cooling, NIC Platform host content ≈ $4,000–$8,000 ASSUMED — DO NOT QUOTE AS FACT
ASSUMED TOTAL LANDED BOM — ≈ $73,000–$99,500, central estimate ≈ $82,000–$88,000 Mixed: one sourced line, four assumed

Supermicro comparison — honest limitation. The brief asks for an equivalent Supermicro system priced and compared. No Supermicro list or street price for an equivalent dual-socket Xeon 6 PCIe GPU platform was obtained, so no delta is stated and none will be invented. What can be said is structural rather than numeric, and it is more useful anyway:

  1. The accelerators are 70–80% of the node, and NVIDIA pricing is broadly common across integrators. The chassis vendor therefore competes over only the remaining 20–30% — roughly $17,000–$29,500 of host content. Chassis-level price competition alone will not move this deal much.
  2. The levers that DO move it are tied to their published constraints: DIMM slot count and 1DPC DDR5-6400 support (they buy half-populated at 16 of 32 slots specifically to preserve upgrade headroom); NVMe bay count (up to 8 bays with boot/data isolation); TDX and SGX enablement out of the box with no reconfiguration, which they market as a differentiator; full IPMI and BIOS access for PXE-booting immutable OS images; and above all thermal and power efficiency per rack, because NTT VA1 caps them at 22 kW per rack and that ceiling — not the purchase price — is what limits how much revenue they can put in a cage.
  3. The genuinely useful comparison for this account is not price-per-box but NODES-PER-RACK WITHIN A 22 kW ENVELOPE.

ACTION: obtain a real configured quote for the equivalent platform and re-run this comparison properly before any customer conversation.

10.4 GAP — cost-ceiling track, stated plainly

  • The rent-derived ceiling is built on an ASSUMED rental band, because OpenMetal publishes no hardware prices at all. Verified across five surfaces. Replace the assumption with a real written quote — they honour quotes for 30 days.
  • Only the GPU line of the BOM is sourced. CPU, memory, NVMe and chassis figures are assumptions and are labelled as such in §10.3.
  • No Supermicro list or street price was obtained for the equivalent platform, so the requested BOM delta is NOT quantified.
  • Historical pricing could not be recovered because Wayback content replay was blocked (§8.4), so no price trend over time exists in this file.
  • The model excludes financing structure, cost of capital, residual value, software subscriptions and support/overhead allocation.

11.2 Network — AS400956

  • Registry: AS400956 (OPENMETAL-AS), ARIN org OPENM-7 "OpenMetal, Inc." — org registration event 2023-05-09T14:05:16-04:00, last changed 2026-02-06T12:23:24-05:00. Holds 21 network objects directly, including IPv6 allocation NET6-2604-1460-1 (OPENMETAL-V6, 2604:1460::/32) (RDAP).
  • Measured BGP footprint (RIPEstat, 2026-08): 32 prefixes — 28 IPv4 totalling 15,616 addresses, plus 2604:1460:2000::/36, 2604:1460:4000::/36, 2604:1460:5000::/36 and 2607:f298:8::/48 (announced-prefixes).
  • ★ Upstreams — and the finding that defines this account's network posture: AS400956 has exactly TWO BGP neighbours, both "left" (upstream), and both are InMotion Hosting: AS22611 (power 881) and AS54641 (power 357). There is no third-party transit and no public peering under OpenMetal's own ASN (asn-neighbours).
  • PeeringDB — a valuable negative: AS400956 is NOT registered in PeeringDB. api/net?asn=400956 returns {"data": [], "meta": {"error": "Entity not found"}}. Its peering is inherited entirely from InMotion Hosting, whose records (org_id 22481) show AS22611 — irr_as_set AS-INMOTION, info_type Content, prefixes4 200, prefixes6 100, policy Selective, ratio Mostly Outbound, ix_count 2, fac_count 3 — and AS54641 — prefixes4 200, prefixes6 50, ix_count 1, fac_count 1. Both poc_updated timestamps are 2020-11-10 and therefore stale (AS22611, AS54641).
  • Customer-facing port capacity (published): 20 Gbps private networking standard on every v4/v5 and GPU node, upgradeable to 40 Gbps; 10 Gbps public on GPU nodes; 6 Gbps public uplink on XL v4; 60 Gbps aggregate mesh on 3-node private cloud clusters, up to 120 Gbps on Medium v5.
  • Egress model: included allowance per server, aggregated across a cluster, then either $375/month per additional 1 Gbps purchased in advance or 95th-percentile billing in arrears (bare metal pricing).
  • Published network uptime: "The network performance for 2022-2025 was >99.99% and is currently tracking at >99.99%. The base SLA is 99.96%."
  • DNS and SaaS stack reinforce the dependency: openmetal.io nameservers are ns1.inmotionhosting.com / ns2.inmotionhosting.com. Mail is Google Workspace (aspmx.l.google.com). SPF is v=spf1 +ip4:198.46.90.88 include:_spf.google.com include:5297785.spf03.hubspotemail.net -all, confirming HubSpot as their marketing automation platform. Domain TXT records also verify Atlassian, Apple, Slack and two Google properties.

★ SALES IMPLICATION: there is no standalone OpenMetal network organisation. Network and facility decisions consolidate back into InMotion's NOC in Virginia Beach — further evidence that the buying centre is Virginia, not Los Angeles.

Lobbying — GAP, and an authoritative one

GAP — NO FEDERAL LOBBYING REGISTRANT EXISTS FOR EITHER COMPANY, AND NO LOBBYING SUB-TABLE IS PRESENTED BECAUSE THERE IS NOTHING TO PUT IN ONE. This is a clean, authoritative negative from the official source, not an inconclusive search. Four separate queries were run against the Lobbying Disclosure Act API operated jointly by the Secretary of the Senate and the Clerk of the House — filings by client_name=OpenMetal, filings by client_name=InMotion, filings by registrant_name=InMotion Hosting, and clients by client_name=OpenMetal. Every one returned exactly {"count":0,"results":[]} (lda.gov API; note that lda.senate.gov/api/v1 301-redirects and must be followed to lda.gov to get JSON). Neither OpenMetal, Inc. nor InMotion Hosting, Inc. has ever appeared as a lobbying client or as a registrant, and neither has retained an outside lobbying firm that filed on its behalf. Reportable lobbying expenditure: $0, because no registration exists — though note that the LDA registration threshold means sub-threshold activity would not appear, so a zero here means no registered lobbying, not necessarily zero contact with government. Interpretation: at a self-declared $6M ARR inside a privately held hosting group with no PAC and no lobbying registration, the company has essentially no federal government-affairs surface. · GAP — OpenSecrets organisation profile: NOT LOCATED for either OpenMetal or InMotion Hosting. Given the LDA result this is the expected outcome, since OpenSecrets derives its federal-lobbying org profiles from LDA filings and there are none to derive from — recorded here so the source is not silently omitted (OpenSecrets methodology).

Bottom line on the political axis, stated plainly. No legislative or policy position of any kind was found for either company or any of its principals. There is no corporate PAC, no lobbying registration, no state-level donation record, and no coordinated corporate vehicle of any kind — the entire federal footprint is $149 of small-dollar individual employee giving across three people under an OpenMetal employer, plus one $25 contribution from an InMotion HR director and a pre-employment $525 from the CTO under a Twilio employer. Nothing here is a wedge and nothing here is a risk. The genuine value of the FEC dataset to this dossier is organisational, not political: it independently verified three OpenMetal employees the website does not fully cover, located an employee the website omits entirely, confirmed the Virginia Beach centre of gravity, and — via the Tischart records — corroborated the CTO's Twilio tenure, job title and Colorado residence from a federal filing.

Surname origin is name-etymology from public onomastic references only — it is NOT a verified statement of any individual's ethnicity or ancestry. Political donations are public record and do not equal party registration.

14. Supermicro sales angle

Classification: Incumbent defence with a live displacement risk

This is neither greenfield nor a straightforward displacement. The evidence says this is an EXISTING Supermicro account that may already have been lost on the current generation, and nobody appears to have noticed.

Supermicro published a case study on this customer group in 2021 (MicroBlade MBE-628E-822 and MBI-6219B-T41N powering InMotion's Flex Metal Cloud, the direct predecessor of OpenMetal), there was a joint press release, and OpenMetal's own website still says today that its XL v2.1 and XL v3 servers "are provisioned on Supermicro's SYS-120C-TN10R." But the current generation was tested directly and the word "Supermicro" appears ZERO times in the raw HTML of the XL v5 page, the H200 GPU page, the GPU pricing page and the full hardware index — while appearing four times each on v2.1 and v3. They named us for two generations and stopped naming anyone from v4 onward. That is the whole opportunity and the whole risk in one fact.

FIRST ACTION IS INTERNAL, NOT EXTERNAL

Before any outreach, check whether an existing account team, prior deal history, or an existing registration covers InMotion Hosting, Inc. or OpenMetal, Inc. The CRM was checked clean for "OpenMetal" — but the 2021 case study was published under INMOTION HOSTING, and the two entities share a street address, a NOC phone block and a BGP path. A clean search on one name proves nothing about the other. Searching only "OpenMetal" and concluding the account is unclaimed would be the single most expensive mistake available here.

Rule 8 — distributor caution (read before dialling)

An account with a published Supermicro case study is almost certainly being served through a distributor or reseller, and the 2021 MicroBlade footprint means there is likely an incumbent channel partner with the relationship. Do not go direct, do not quote around the channel, and do not register this until the distributor position is established. If a partner already owns the account, the correct move is to bring them the v5/GPU refresh intelligence and co-sell — the value added here is the timing and the technical wedge, not the right to transact.

Also note that the group's stated partner list allegedly includes Dell alongside Supermicro. That is unverified (§6.1) — but if true there is an incumbent competitor already inside the tent.

The one qualifying question for first contact

Ask this of Jamie Tischart (CTO), and ask it exactly once, early:

"Your site still says the XL v2.1 and v3 were provisioned on Supermicro SYS-120C-TN10R, but the v4, v5 and the new H200 and RP6000 pages don't name a platform vendor at all. What changed at v4, and who is building the GPU nodes today?"

That single question resolves the incumbency status, surfaces the competitor if there is one, tells you whether this is defence or displacement — and does it without revealing that you do not already know.

The four levers that actually matter to this buyer, in order

  1. POWER DENSITY, NOT PRICE PER BOX. Their only GPU site, NTT VA1 in Ashburn, is capped at 22 kW per rack. At roughly 2–2.5 kW for a dual-H200-NVL node they get about 8–10 nodes per rack. They are a colocation TENANT at Digital Realty and NTT with no owned-facility advantage, so every additional revenue-generating node per rack is pure margin to them. Lead with nodes-per-rack inside a 22 kW envelope, not unit price — and note their own top case study is literally titled "How a Public Cloud Provider Increased Density, Modernized Hardware, and Slashed Costs." Density is a story they already sell downstream.
  2. THE ACCELERATOR IS 70–80% OF THE BOM, SO CHASSIS PRICE IS NOT THE BATTLEGROUND. Two H200 NVL cards at $28k–$35k street dominate a roughly $73k–$99k node. Competing on host cost fights over $17k–$29k. Compete instead on their published technical non-negotiables: 32 DIMM slots at 1DPC DDR5-6400 (they deliberately populate only 16 of 32 to preserve headroom); up to 8 NVMe bays with boot/data isolation; TDX and SGX enabled out of the box with no reconfiguration; full IPMI and BIOS access for PXE-booting immutable images like Talos and Flatcar; and their unusual published interest in an attested GPU inside an Intel TDX trust domain.
  3. ONE PLATFORM WIN CAPTURES THE WHOLE CATALOGUE. Medium v5, Large v5, XL v5, the H200 SKU and the RP6000 SKU all run the same dual-socket Xeon 6 host, differentiated only by CPU SKU, DIMM population, drive count and accelerator. This is not a SKU sale, it is a platform standardisation sale — and correspondingly, losing the platform loses everything at once.
  4. TIMING IS THE STRONGEST CARD. The current GPU generation went GA on 2026-06-17 into Ashburn ONLY, with "additional locations planned" and "Contact us about availability in other regions" in writing. The previous generation shipped to US East AND US West on day one, so West Coast GPU demand is already proven. Los Angeles LAX10, Amsterdam and Singapore are three built-out sites with zero current-generation GPU. That expansion — not a generational refresh — is the near-term purchase, and it plausibly lands Q4 2026 to Q2 2027.

Right-size the pursuit and do not over-invest

They self-disclose $6M ARR on their own careers page, targeting $18M. Their bare metal FAQ says they keep "up to 12 of a particular type in a particular data center" on hand and that initial customer deployments of "3 and 6 are common." This is a buyer who transacts in units of roughly 3–12 nodes on a repeating cadence with long price-lock tails, not a hundred-node tender. It will not clear a $100M threshold and should not be resourced as though it might.

Its real value is annuity and reference: a fast-follower on Intel silicon (Granite Rapids within about 15 months), a visible OpenInfra Foundation member and OpenStack Foundation corporate sponsor, and a company whose customers are themselves cloud and hosting providers — so the logo compounds.

Use their own words on cost — and heed their own warning

They published on 2026-05-15 that server DRAM rose nearly 95% in early 2026, that "this affects every infrastructure provider including OpenMetal", that "OpenMetal's new v5 hardware reflects current market pricing", and pointedly that "anyone telling you they've found a way around the supply crunch is worth scrutinizing carefully."

That last line is a warning. Do not walk in claiming to have beaten the supply crunch — they will see straight through it and they have said so in public. Lead instead with supply certainty, delivery-date reliability against their own promise that servers are "delivered on schedule or your first month free", and density economics — the three things a margin-squeezed colo tenant on a fixed-price rental model actually needs.

⚠️ Finally, and most importantly — re-test the territory before registering

The brief assumes Los Angeles / T1. That could not be substantiated. OpenMetal, Inc.'s HQ is 2 Constitution Drive, Suite 101, Virginia Beach, VA 23462, confirmed by both the site footer and ARIN. Every press release carries a Virginia Beach dateline. The CTO, CFO, CLO and President all sit on the East Coast, the GPU fleet is in Ashburn, and the network terminates in InMotion's Virginia NOC. Los Angeles is a leased Digital Realty cage with no current-generation GPU in it. The only real California nexus is Sash Ghosh in Santa Monica and a 2004-era InMotion ARIN registration at 2800 28th St.

A T1 registration on this account looks contestable and could be challenged by an East Coast team. Escalate the territory question before spending pipeline credibility on it.

Sequence — do not reorder

① Clear the channel/distributor position under Rule 8, searching BOTH "OpenMetal, Inc." and "InMotion Hosting, Inc." → ② Escalate and resolve the territory question against the Virginia Beach HQ → ③ Register only after ① and ② → ④ Make contact via Sash Ghosh (323) 301-7563 or the Partnership Inquiry route, and ask Jamie Tischart the single qualifying question above.

OpenMetal（InMotion Hosting） — 銷售情報檔案

編製對象： Supermicro 銷售一組（美國）· 專員 US8664 Tuo Cheng · 日期： 2026-08-11 方法： 以公司歷史、財務與登記資料、美國政治傾向、法案與政策立場，以及該公司與其客戶之關係為研究軸線。本公司為私有持股，證據來自各州商業登記、ARIN／PeeringDB、業者自行公布之 GPU 型錄與定價、UCC 歸檔、徵才啟事、社群論壇及 FEC 紀錄。每項事實均隨附來源與日期。GAP = 查證資料中未找到，不得憑記憶補寫。 轄區： 加州洛杉磯 — West Coast South California = T1｜T2。一組可直接註冊。 CRM 狀態： 2026-08-11 於 salesleads Search（Type = All）實查為乾淨 — 無 lead、無 account、無 do-not-call。一組可註冊。

1. 結論摘要

OpenMetal, Inc. 是一家獨立設立的法人，經營 OpenStack 私有雲、裸機（bare metal）與 GPU 服務，隸屬於 InMotion Hosting 集團，總部設於 2 Constitution Drive, Suite 101, Virginia Beach, VA 23462（官網頁尾，載明「2022-2026 © OpenMetal, Inc.」；同一地址亦為 InMotion Hosting, Inc. 於 ARIN 登記之地址，org 代號 INMOT-1，登記於 2008-06-03）。該公司在自家現行徵才頁上自行揭露 $6M ARR，目標 $18M（OpenMetal careers）——這是本檔中最有價值、來源等級也最高的商業數據，因為它是第一手自行揭露，不是資料商估計。

這是一個既有的 Supermicro 帳戶，而且很可能已經在當世代被丟掉了，卻沒有人注意到。 OpenMetal 自家現行網站至今仍寫著其 XL v2.1 與 XL v3 裸機伺服器「are provisioned on Supermicro's SYS-120C-TN10R」（XL v2.1、XL v3；頁面 metadata 之 dateModified 為 2026-01-23，代表此說法仍在維護中，並非陳舊殘留）；而 Supermicro 自家公布的案例研究（© 2021）亦具名 MBE-628E-822 MicroBlade 機箱搭配 MBI-6219B-T41N 刀鋒節點，用於支撐 InMotion 的 Flex Metal Cloud——也就是 OpenMetal 的直系前身（Supermicro 案例研究 PDF）。然而，針對 XL v5 頁、H200 GPU 頁、GPU 定價頁 與完整硬體索引的原始 HTML 進行的直接負向測試顯示：「Supermicro」出現 0 次、「SYS-」型號字串出現 0 次，而 v2.1 與 v3 各出現 4 次並附型號。他們替我們具名了兩個世代，從 v4 起就不再具名任何人。 這一個不對稱，同時就是機會、風險，以及第一個該問的問題。

撥號前有兩件事必須先釐清。第一是通路。 一個有公開 Supermicro 案例研究的客戶，幾乎確定是透過經銷商或代理商服務，2021 年的 MicroBlade 足跡也暗示已有既有通路夥伴。CRM 對「OpenMetal」實查為乾淨——但 2021 年那份案例研究是掛在 InMotion Hosting 名下發表的，而兩個法人共用同一街道地址、同一組 NOC 電話號段與同一條 BGP 路徑。用其中一個名字查乾淨，完全不能證明另一個名字也乾淨。 第二是轄區。 需求書把本案放在洛杉磯／T1；證據並不支持。洛杉磯是租用的 Digital Realty LAX10 機櫃區，而且裡面沒有任何當世代 GPU；企業決策中心、CTO、CFO、CLO、總裁、GPU 機隊與網路全部都在維吉尼亞。請見 §2 與 §14 的轄區警告——註冊前先上報。

商業上請務必合理估量規模。這是一個每次 3 至 12 台、反覆下單、帶有長期價格鎖定尾巴的買家（裸機定價 FAQ：「we typically can deploy up to 12 of a particular type in a particular data center at any given time」；初期部署「can be of any size but 3 and 6 are common」），不是上百台的大型標案，且公開資料完全不支持 $100M+ 的定性。它的價值在於年金型營收與參考指標：Medium v5、Large v5、XL v5 與兩個 GPU SKU 共用同一套主機平台，因此一次平台標準化的勝出即可拿下整份型錄——反過來說，輸掉平台就是一次全輸。

2. 公司速覽

欄位 內容 證據／日期
法人名稱 OpenMetal, Inc. — 為獨立法人，並非 InMotion Hosting 的產品線名稱。無 SEC 申報（私有持股） 法人名稱由公司自行公布於官網頁尾（「2022-2026 © OpenMetal, Inc.」）及商標公告頁的法務聯絡管道上方（最後更新 2025-07-08）。ARIN 亦以此名稱登記 org 代號 OPENM-7（RDAP）
與 InMotion Hosting 之關係 InMotion 集團內的兄弟／子品牌 — 在網路與基礎設施上依附 InMotion，並共用其 Virginia Beach 總部地址。此關係由四條互相獨立的軸線確立 （1）公司面：About 頁載明「OpenMetal was founded by the team behind InMotion Hosting」；Todd Robinson 同為兩家公司的共同創辦人暨總裁；451 Research 稱其為 InMotion Hosting 的「spinout」。（2）網路面： OpenMetal 有自己的 ARIN org 與自己的 ASN（AS400956），但 AS400956 僅有兩個 BGP 上游，且兩個都是 InMotion — AS22611 與 AS54641，沒有任何第三方 transit（RIPEstat asn-neighbours）。（3）DNS 面： openmetal.io 的 nameserver 是 ns1.inmotionhosting.com／ns2.inmotionhosting.com。（4）電話面： OpenMetal 的 ARIN POC 電話 +1-757-693-5498 與 InMotion NOC 的 +1-757-693-5293 落在同一個 757-693-5xxx 號段（RDAP autnum 22611）
設立州別 GAP — 未經查證。不得斷言任何一州。 沒有任何一個州務卿登記系統可由程式觸及（完整紀錄見 §9.3）。證據指向維吉尼亞州為營運／總部所在州，但在德拉瓦州設立、再於維州辦理外州登記的可能性同樣合理，且未經證實。目前唯一近似登記性質的證據是 ARIN org 紀錄 OPENM-7——而 ARIN 是網路號碼登錄機構，不是公司登記機關
總部（實查地址） 2 Constitution Drive, Suite 101, Virginia Beach, VA 23462 逐字取自 openmetal.io 頁尾。由 ARIN org INMOT-1 獨立佐證——「InMotion Hosting, Inc.」，同一街道地址，登記於 2008-06-03。OpenMetal 兩則新聞稿皆為「VIRGINIA BEACH, Va.」發稿地（2025-04-11、2026-06-17）
成立年份 品牌／產品 2021；取得自有 ARIN org 為 2023-05-09 About 頁：「In 2021, the conversation around cloud costs started shifting」。頁尾著作權為「2022-2026」。前身產品為 InMotion 的 Flex Metal Cloud，2021 年已上線（Supermicro 案例研究，© 2021）。依公開檔案彙整（次級來源），Todd Robinson 自 2019 年 11 月起即為 OpenMetal.io 總裁。ARIN org OPENM-7 登記事件為 2023-05-09T14:05:16-04:00，最後異動 2026-02-06——請注意這是取得自有 ARIN org 的日期，不是公司設立日
所有權 外觀為創辦人自有；查無任何募資輪、投資人、股權結構或集團控股實體 — GAP 未找到任何募資公告。About 頁將公司定位為由創辦人自 InMotion 營運經驗中長出來的事業。CRO 職缺中揭露存在「OpenMetal Board」（careers），但其成員一位都不具名 — GAP
員工數 團隊頁具名 13 人 ＋ 至少 1 位未列於該頁但有聯邦申報佐證之員工 ＋ 1 個高階空缺。 母集團為「125,000+ 客戶、350+ 團隊成員」 團隊頁列出 13 人。Mike Mayer（Software Engineer，密西根州 Ferndale）僅出現在以 OpenMetal 為雇主的 FEC 申報中——因此實際人數超過 13 人。集團數字逐字取自 Todd Robinson 個人頁：品牌包含 InMotion Hosting、BoldGrid、Web Hosting Hub、Ramnode
營收 $6M ARR，目標 $18M — 第一手自行揭露，非估計值 逐字取自現行 CRO 職缺：「We're looking for a hands-on Chief Revenue Officer (CRO) to support scaling our cloud infrastructure company from $6M ARR to $18M ARR」（careers）。範圍註記： 此為 OpenMetal 品牌，非 InMotion 集團，後者規模明顯更大。未找到集團層級營收數字 — GAP。 針對 CRM $100M 門檻的判定：公開資料不支持 $100M+ 之定性

⚠️ 轄區警告 — 註冊前必讀

洛杉磯／T1 這個前提不被查證結果支持，本帳戶的 T1 註冊看起來是可被挑戰的。

  • 洛杉磯是租用的主機代管據點，既不是辦公室、也不是總部。 OpenMetal 是 Digital Realty LAX10 的承租戶（設施規格頁）。本次要談的 GPU 伺服器目前只部署在維吉尼亞州 Ashburn（GPU 定價頁：「The RTX PRO 6000 and H200 are available now from OpenMetal US East in Ashburn, Virginia」）。
  • 企業決策中心在維吉尼亞州 Virginia Beach。 總部地址、每一則新聞稿的發稿地，以及總裁、CTO、CFO、CLO 全部在東岸。網路終結於 InMotion 位於維州的 NOC。
  • 真正存在的加州連結很窄，但確實存在：（a）另一個更早的 ARIN org「InMotion Hosting」（代號 INMOT），登記於 2004-06-14，地址 2800 28th St., Suite 321, Santa Monica, CA 90405；（b）Sash Ghosh，Head of Growth and Partnerships，依其本人 FEC 申報位於加州 Santa Monica，且在 OpenMetal 新聞稿上使用 (323) 洛杉磯區碼電話；（c）InMotion Hosting 於 2001 年的第一座資料中心位於洛杉磯（Todd Robinson 個人頁）。
  • 行動建議： 註冊前，以 Virginia Beach 總部重跑一次轄區判定。本案讀起來像是「東岸持有的 T2 帳戶 ＋ 一個加州衛星據點」，T1 註冊可能被挑戰。

3. 原始名單更正表

判定等級：confirmed（成立）＝第一手具名揭露，或多方獨立佐證｜partly confirmed（部分成立）＝核心屬實，但表述方式須修正｜contradicted（與證據相悖）＝證據與原始名單相反｜unverified（未經證實）＝無法由公開來源確立，不得向客戶陳述。

第 3 至 9 列與第 14 列更正的是本檔前一輪自己的說法，不是原始需求書。這些內容在此明列而非默默覆寫，因為其中有四項會改變這個帳戶的實際操作方式——特別是第 3 列的聯絡管道更正，它推翻了前一輪「OpenMetal 沒有任何個人公布私人 email」的結論。

# 說法 判定 證據 來源
1 需求書把 OpenMetal 定位為可由「AS22611（InMotion Hosting）」觸及 partly confirmed AS22611 確實是 InMotion Hosting, Inc. 的 ASN — handle AS22611、名稱 INMOTION、org 為 InMotion Hosting Inc（INMOT-1），登記於 2012-11-02，最後異動 2020-10-28。但它不是 OpenMetal 的 ASN。OpenMetal, Inc. 持有自己的 AS400956，名稱 OPENMETAL-AS，登記人 OPENM-7，登記於 2024-04-04，並配有自己的 OMI 職務帳號 POC。只用 AS22611 界定 OpenMetal 的人，看到的是母公司的網路，不是標的公司的網路。 兩者確實相連——但透過的是 BGP 依附關係，不是共用 ASN RDAP autnum 400956 · RDAP autnum 22611
2 業界媒體與兩家公司自身溝通中普遍出現的說法：OpenMetal 是「InMotion Hosting 的一個事業部」，亦即並非獨立法人 contradicted OpenMetal, Inc. 是獨立設立的法人，「事業部」這個框架的不精確之處，正好決定了採購訂單由誰簽署。四項獨立佐證：（1） 它以自己的法人名義簽署法律文件——商標公告載明「OpenMetal, Inc.（"Company"）reserves the right…」，並將侵權主張導向「OpenMetal, Inc., Attn: Legal Department」；隱私權政策以「OPENMETAL, INC.」名義簽署；網站著作權標示為「© OpenMetal, Inc.」。（2） 它持有自己的 ARIN 組織 OPENM-7 與自己的 ASN AS400956。（3） 它是 openmetal.io 的具名登記人（登記組織「OpenMetal, Inc.」）。（4） 它維護自己的 POC 代號 OOT1-ARIN 與 OAT5-ARIN，兩者的公司欄位皆標示「OMI …（OpenMetal, Inc.）」。真正的事業部不會具備上述任何一項。正確描述：在共同創辦人所有權之下、獨立設立的營運關係企業，具交錯董事會與共用的 CFO／董事長——不是合資（任何紀錄中都沒有第三方夥伴），也不是 d/b/a 商號 商標公告 · RDAP openmetal.io
3 本檔前一輪：「沒有任何一位具名人員公布個人 email。除 Sash Ghosh 的媒體電話外，全部是部門管道（sales@、legal@、noc@、abuse@）。」 contradicted — 本檔最具實務後果的一項更正 OpenMetal 十二個團隊個人頁中，有十個直接在頁面上公布個人 email： raym@openmetal.io（Raymond Meyers, CFO）、yuriys@openmetal.io、jacobh@openmetal.io、morh@openmetal.io、rafaelh@openmetal.io、sashg@openmetal.io、madeleinef@openmetal.io、ramong@openmetal.io、peterl@openmetal.io、laurenm@openmetal.io。只有 Todd Robinson 與 Jamie Tischart 未公布個人信箱。 前一輪應是讀取了算繪後的頁面，而非 mailto: href。關鍵推論：這些信箱的本地部（local part）並不遵循單一可猜規則——Rafael Ramos 是 rafaelh@、Madeleine Ethridge 是 madeleinef@，都不對應顯示姓氏——因此必須完全照公布內容使用，絕不可外推到未公布信箱的那兩位高階主管 Raymond Meyers 個人頁
4 前一輪：「僅浮現兩個 LinkedIn 網址（Robinson、Ghosh），且皆為間接搜尋所得、未直接抓取。Tischart、Shyyan、Morley 皆無可查證網址。」 contradicted OpenMetal 自家 careers 頁上，有四個 LinkedIn 個人檔案網址是以 href 形式寫死在頁面裡，因此屬公司公布之一手紀錄，而非搜尋猜測：toddrobinson2（貼文 ugcPost-7477405620271452160）、tischart（ugcPost-7485004324532551680）、yuriy-shyyan（ugcPost-7483148275387187200）、lauren-morley（activity-7480969045744234496）。次階發現： 這四人正是公司指定的招募發聲者，也就是誰在對外代表 OpenMetal 說話。Sash Ghosh 的 LinkedIn 仍為未經證實——她的個人頁與 careers 頁皆未出現，故前一輪由搜尋推得的網址在此不予採信 careers 頁
5 前一輪：Mor Hanukaev 職稱衝突未解——「未經查證前不得對他使用任何職稱」，團隊頁索引寫「Director of Sales」、個人頁寫「Director of Cloud Services」 partly confirmed 衝突確實存在且仍在線上，但現在已可判定，不再是僵局。他本人的個人頁在兩個互相獨立的欄位上一致：H-tag 為「Director of Cloud Services」，頁面 meta description 為「Meet Mor Hanukaev, Director of Cloud Services for OpenMetal.」同一頁兩個欄位的份量高於索引頁的一行，故團隊頁索引的「Director of Sales」是離群值。同一網站另發現一項先前未報告的新職稱衝突： Sash Ghosh 頁面 H-tag 為「Head of Growth and Partnerships」，其 meta description 卻為「Head of Growth Strategy and Analyst Relations」。OpenMetal 的頁面 metadata 普遍落後於其可見內容 Mor Hanukaev 個人頁
6 前一輪：團隊頁列出 13 人；員工數為「具名 13 人 ＋ 至少 1 位有聯邦文件佐證但未列名者」 partly confirmed 依 page-sitemap 列舉，個人頁實為 12 個而非 13 個：Robinson、Meyers、Tischart、Shyyan、Ramos、Hanukaev、Hipps、Ghosh、Ethridge、Grullon、Lee、Morley。不是名冊縮編，就是前一輪重複計算。「至少一位未列名員工」一點則成立且可再延伸：Mike Mayer（Software Engineer，密西根 Ferndale）僅見於 FEC 申報；Timothy Monner 僅見於過時頁面 metadata 與舊新聞稿署名。實際員工數超過公開名冊，且該名冊不是可靠的人數普查 page-sitemap.xml
7 前一輪：「GAP — 採購／採購主管／供應商管理：查無此職稱……採購權似乎未下放，落在 CTO／CFO／President 手上」 confirmed 成立，並已進一步收斂。OpenMetal 團隊頁、InMotion Hosting 的 Senior Leadership Team 與 Board of Directors 區塊皆無任何採購、購買或供應商管理職稱。但推論現在是具體的而非泛泛的：採購權集中於 Raymond Meyers，他同時是 OpenMetal 的 CFO 暨 Head of M&A，以及 InMotion Hosting 的 Chief Administrative Officer 暨 Board Chair。前一輪的「CTO／CFO／President」方向正確；真正的發現是財務席位與董事長席位是同一個人 inmotionhosting.com About
8 前一輪：「GAP — OpenMetal Board 成員：董事會的存在已在 CRO 職缺中揭露，但一位成員都沒有具名」 partly confirmed OpenMetal 的董事仍然一位都沒有具名，該缺口維持。 但InMotion Hosting 的董事會現已在母公司 About 頁上完整列出——Todd Robinson（President & Board Member）、Sunil Saxena（Vice President & Board Member）、Ray Meyers（Chief Administrative Officer & Board Chair）、Dan Cunningham（Chief Revenue Officer & Board Member）。由於 Robinson 與 Meyers 分別握有 OpenMetal 的 President 與 CFO 席位，InMotion 董事會是 OpenMetal 重大資本採購最可能的實質核准機關——但這是由席位重疊推得的推論，任何紀錄都未如此記載，不得當作事實陳述 inmotionhosting.com About
9 前一輪：「『Tom Callon, CEO of OpenMetal』僅為次級來源、無佐證、已排除。不得使用此姓名。」 confirmed 本輪獨立重測，排除決定維持。「Tom Callon」不出現在 OpenMetal 團隊頁、page-sitemap 列舉的十二個個人頁網址、InMotion 的 Senior Leadership Team 或 Board of Directors 區塊、ARIN 與 PeeringDB 紀錄，或任何一筆 FEC 申報中。該排除指示正確，在此再次確認 page-sitemap.xml
10 標的框架中隱含的業界假設：InMotion Hosting 的雲端產品與 OpenMetal 是同一條產品線，因此一次雲端對話可同時觸及兩者 contradicted InMotion Hosting 另行行銷自己的雲端產品——「Managed Private Cloud」與「InMotion Cloud」，其網站描述為「our managed private cloud, built on dedicated infrastructure and operated by the same engineering team that runs everything else」——與 OpenMetal 並行且有所區別。集團在 InMotion 這一側另設GM, Cloud Division（Jason Loucks），而 OpenMetal 則有自己的 CTO 與 Director of Cloud Systems Architecture。同一集團內至少有兩條雲端市場路線與兩條技術領導鏈。 Loucks 的 Cloud Division 職權範圍是否正式涵蓋 OpenMetal，任何來源皆未載明，在此列為未決問題 inmotionhosting.com About
11 InMotion Hosting 或 OpenMetal 作為法人具有政治活動，或員工捐款反映公司立場 contradicted 三項各自獨立的負向證據。（1） FEC 沒有以任一公司名義登記的企業 PAC。（2） 沒有聯邦遊說登記——官方 LDA API 在四種查詢寫法下皆回傳 count=0。（3） 整體聯邦足跡都是小額個人員工捐款：以 OpenMetal 為雇主者共 7 筆、3 人、合計 $149，另有較廣的 InMotion 雇主索引，成員為系統管理員、QA 分析師與會計人員。唯一看似州級「政治」命中的紀錄其實是陷阱： VPAP 將 InMotion Hosting 列為收受 $5,020 的供應商（VENDOR），款項來自 11 個維州政治委員會、用於網站與電子報服務——錢是流向公司，不是流出公司。這是商業交易，不帶任何政治傾向意涵，絕不可被描述為捐款 VPAP 供應商紀錄
12 InMotion Hosting 自家 About 頁的說法：其於「California, Virginia, and the Netherlands」設有資料中心 unverified 無法由網路登錄資料證實，且與其部分抵觸。InMotion 的 PeeringDB 紀錄僅列三處設施：Digital Realty LAX（600 W 7th）、Digital Realty AMS3/5/7/8/10（阿姆斯特丹）、Digital Realty Singapore SIN10，以及兩處交換中心（MegaIX Los Angeles 100 Gbps、ERA-IX Amsterdam 10 Gbps）。加州與荷蘭獲得佐證；維吉尼亞並未列出，而 About 頁未提的新加坡卻有列出。 這也可能只代表 PeeringDB 紀錄陳舊（其 poc_updated 為 2020-11-10），但落差確實存在，在談容量之前值得先釐清任何據點說法 PeeringDB AS22611
13 由 Virginia Beach 總部推得 OpenMetal, Inc. 的設立州別為維吉尼亞州 unverified 任何一州皆未取得 OpenMetal, Inc. 的登記紀錄，故設立州別確實不明。本輪唯一的方向性訊號反而指向維州以外：openmetal.io 的 RDAP 登記紀錄載明登記組織「OpenMetal, Inc.」、登記人所在州為 CALIFORNIA。這與集團的加州起源一致——InMotion Hosting 是加州公司（entity C2456851），登記地址在 El Segundo，其較早的 ARIN org INMOT 於 2004 年登記於 Santa Monica，第一座資料中心也在洛杉磯。但網域登記人地址不等於設立州別的證明。 加州、維州與德拉瓦州三者皆仍有可能 RDAP openmetal.io
14 前一輪：七人的 FEC 個別姓名查詢因 DEMO_KEY 觸及 OVER_RATE_LIMIT 而未完成，無法排除以不同或空白雇主申報的可能 confirmed 該限制確實存在，且現已就多數姓名解除——改用 FEC 在自家 fec.gov/data 頁面原始碼中公布的公開 Schedule A key 重跑。結果：Shyyan 與 Hanukaev 在全庫回傳零筆（罕見姓氏，近乎決定性）；Hipps 3 筆，全為 2009 年芝加哥 Sears Holdings 財務人員；Morley 14 筆，全為其他女性；Grullon 76 筆，無雇主吻合。Ramos（799 筆） 與 Peter Lee 為常見姓名，這兩人的負向結論仍較弱，且如實標示、不誇大。淨結果：七人中五人已明確解決，兩人受姓名常見度限制 FEC — SHYYAN, YURIY
15 Biden Victory Fund 與 Harris Victory Fund 是兩個不同的受贈機構 contradicted 兩者是同一個 FEC 委員會 C00744946，在不同選舉週期使用兩個名稱。首次申報為 2020-04-24 之 BIDEN VICTORY FUND（司庫 Joseph W.
